    - **From Tier VI (`GAMBLER_TIER`, campaign gambler.ts) a Shrine is the Gambler's Shrine:** take **two different** blessings (`GAMBLER_BLESSINGS`) of seven. The three quiet ones (Rest, Upgrade, Let one go) and four wagers: **Sacrifice** (a card of yours burns for one of three Rift-crossed cards on the altar: a rare and two uncommons, three rares from Tier X), **Loaded Dice** (sanctify one card you choose: 60% it rises 25% at Tier VI, +2 points a tier to 80%, otherwise it falls 25%), **the Chisel** (engraves the shrine's keyword, Hold, Opening hand or Flare, rolled per visit, onto a card) and **the Ledger** (+4 Max HP, and 2 more each time it is signed this run: 4, 6, 8...; `RunState.ledger` counts). The Ledger is a shrine blessing, not a Boon: nothing stacks on the Boon bar, and it costs one of the visit's two blessings each time.
    - **The Brass Reel** (the elite events' slot machine, `GAMBLER_REEL`) stands beside the blessings: 25 shards a spin, one prize a visit; taking it does not use a blessing and the shrine stays open.
    - **Walk away any time:** every Shrine (quiet or Gambler's) now has a Walk away / Leave button (`leaveShrine`).
    - **Baseline:** every Tier table row +15 HP and +8 damage points (Tier I 130% / 120%, Tier XX 775% / 384%); the step sizes are unchanged, so the ladder still only rises.
    - Why these: each wager is a different kind of growth (a new card from another class, a gamble on raw numbers, a keyword, sturdiness), they scale with the tier or with the run, and two of seven makes every visit a choice. The quiet shrine stays as it was below Tier VI.

235. **A ghost must have cleared Tier 3 (Robert, 2026-10-25: "ghost decks should be at least tier 3 cleared, lets remove any that havent cleared tier 3 and are below") →** `ghostCleared(tier, stage)` (campaign mapgen): Tier IV or above, or Tier III with all three stages cleared (a deck with no stage recorded at Tier III does not count). It gates every ghost: the Arena's `pickGhost`, the Warden `pickGhostBoss`, `checkGhostBoss` on the client, the `current_ghosts` view (the Ghost decks page), and `registerDeck` (a deck that has not cleared Tier 3 still plays in the Arena but is not kept as a ghost). The board Durable Object deletes the ghost rows that do not qualify when it starts (`UNCLEARED_GHOSTS`); their match history stays.

236. **No Boons in the Arena (Robert, 2026-10-25: "lets disallow boons in pvp. i noticed sehra did 150+ damage turn one in pvp") →** reverses #171. `setupFor` (`@sr/pvp`) gives both seats no Boons, so every Arena match (person against person, or against a ghost, on the server and in the browser) is cards only. A deck still carries its run's Boons: the Saved decks report shows them as "not used in the Arena", the Arena list no longer counts them, and a ghost Warden in the campaign (a campaign fight, not an Arena match) still has them. Open: Arena HP is still 3x a Riftwalker's base (`ARENA_HP_MULT`), set before the balance pass; burst like Sehra's Radiant (triple damage) may still end a match in two or three turns, so this is the next number to look at.

237. **Arena HP, no one-shots, ghost Wardens that grow, Max HP from Wardens (Robert, 2026-10-25: "increase the player hp but i do want riftwalker's damage numbers to be similar ... i absolutely dont want decreased damage numbers in arena. we need to make sure to offer enough max hp increases in campaign. not sure if we should simimlarly increase minions hp in arena, ill leave that up to you"; "arena decks also shouldnt show any boons"; "make sure ghosts in campaign get similar hp/dmg boosts as regular bosses in different tiers"; "re-evaluate the balance of daamge from characters so pvp can be ok instead of one-shots ... even one shots sholdnt happen") →** measured first, then changed (tools: `pnpm audit:balance -- burst|optimize --noboons`, and the new `tools/src/arenasim.ts`).
    - **What was found:** with no Boons, the deck optimizer (what a person hunting a combo converges on) found turn-one ceilings at Tier X of 333 (Totemist, Spirit stacking: three Fevered Communion+), 296 (Hunter, four Blood Tithe+ drawing the deck), 183 (Sehra, Radiant), 150 (Trapper), the rest 50-135; the Arena HP was 3x base (210-264). Bot-drafted decks never one-shot (their biggest turn p90 is 40-50% of the old Arena HP).
    - **Arena HP 6x base** (`ARENA_HP_MULT` 3 -> 6, 420-528; going second +60): damage is untouched. In the bot sim matches go from a median 8 rounds to about 12 (p10 7).
    - **Two engines trimmed** (campaign too): Fevered Communion Spirit 3/4 -> 2/3; Blood Tithe+ loses 4 HP, +2 Mana, draws 3 (was lose 5, draw 4; the base card is unchanged). Turn-one ceilings after: Totemist 263 (about 63% of the lowest Arena HP, an Engineer's 420), Hunter 188, everyone else under 190. At Tier XX the search found Sehra at 355 (Radiant triples a turn, and entering it paid back 2 Mana, so a Rise Radiant+ at 2 cost nothing): **entering Radiant now gives 1 Mana** (`RADIANT_ENTER_MANA` 2 -> 1; it still triples, and the tooltip now says so), which brings her to about 326, 78% of the lowest Arena HP and 62% of the highest. (Tried and not taken: Radiant x2.5, 296, which takes her identity; Rise Radiant+ Holding instead of costing 2, 346, since Burning Litany enters Radiant too; no Mana at all, 275, which costs her too much in the campaign.) Other Tier XX ceilings: Barbarian lane 0 208, Assassin 155, Trapper 135, Engineer 130, the rest under 125.
    - **Minions keep their HP:** their damage is unchanged, so they last as many hits as before; scaling them with hero HP would make ally decks into walls.
    - **No Boons on Arena decks:** the Saved decks report no longer shows them (they are still saved with the deck, for a ghost Warden).
    - **A rules version for the Arena** (`RULES_VERSION` in `@sr/pvp`, now 2): a browser sends it when it queues, asks for a ghost or joins a room, and the server refuses a different one ("The game has been updated. Reload the page to play."); a room remembers the version its game was started under, and a game saved under another is ended (a draw) instead of being replayed under new numbers. Raise it whenever a change alters how a match plays out.
    - **Ghost Wardens grow like Wardens:** HP = its Riftwalker's base x 1.3 (`GHOST_BOSS_HP_BASE`) x how far the tier table's HP has risen since Tier IV stage 1, and its damage (a new `HeroSetup.dmgPct`, which also scales its allies) rises the same way from 100% there (`ghostBossHp`, `ghostBossDmg`, by tier and stage). Not the table's whole percent: a ghost is a full Riftwalker's deck that already hits like a player, and the full table made it nearly unbeatable (1-8% for the bot). Measured with `arenasim --ghost`: the bot wins about as often as against the usual Warden (Tier V ~45%, X ~13%, XX ~14%; the usual Warden 44%, 15%, 14%). Before, ghosts got much too easy deeper in (31% at Tier X, 40% at XX).
    - **Max HP from every Warden:** beating a Warden gives +3 Max HP for good (`wardenMaxHp`: 3 to Tier IV, 4 from V, 5 from X, 6 from XV, 7 at XX), so a run that keeps going grows with its enemies; it is told as the stop's news. With the Ledger (#234), events and Boons, Max HP now keeps up with depth.

238. **A Store, Arena trophies, deck limits, more events, ghost Wardens on the map (Robert, 2026-10-25: "a Store page like any other game ... a button to link to it from the homescreen and some kind of icon associated with purchases ... a cosmetic store ... content coming soon"; "a trophy case for weekly and monthly rewards ... top 100 of players in arena ... a weekly trophy and monthly trophy at the end of those cycles ... shows on their player cards in arena with a mini trophies and the number ... weekly/monthly should be different"; "a limit on ancestral cards in a deck ... only allow one legendary per deck and have some events that allow for sacrificing a legendary for another one, potentially a rift crossed one that they can choose to swap or keep"; "more interesting events in general"; "ghost bosses in campaign are clearly indicated on the map") →**
    - **Store** (`#/store`, `apps/web/src/app/StoreScreen.tsx`): a home button with the Store's mark (`StoreIcon`: a satchel with a rift crystal, meant to stand for anything bought there) and a page of shelves (card backs, Riftwalker looks, battlefields, card frames, titles and banners, emotes), each "Coming soon", saying it will be cosmetic only. No currency, prices or payment yet (that is Robert's call: monetisation).
    - **Arena trophies:** when a week or month of the Arena board ends, its top 100 (`TROPHY_PLACES`, with at least one win) each get that period's trophy, kept for good in a `trophies` table of the Leaderboard object. It is settled lazily: the first request after a period ends gives them out (each period once, a kv flag; every period ended since the last one settled, so a quiet week is not skipped), from the results the board still keeps (70 days). The room asks for counts before it seats a player, with a one-second limit, so a slow board never holds up or tangles a join. A weekly trophy is a silver cup, a monthly one a larger gold cup with a star (`TrophyCup`); counts show beside a player's name on the fight's name tags, the "who goes first" cards and the Arena boards (`TrophyBadges`). The room asks the board for a joining player's counts (by their leaderboard id) and puts them on the public entry; whatever a browser sends is dropped by `checkEntry`. **Trophy case** (`#/trophies`, home link and the Leaderboards page): totals and every trophy with its week or month and place (`POST /pvp/board/trophies`). (The first deploy settles the week and month just gone, so their top 100 get trophies at once.) The Leaderboards page said boards close at UTC midnight; they close at Pacific midnight, and now say so.
    - **Deck limits:** one Legendary (`maxLegendary` 2 -> 1) and at most two Ancestral cards (`maxAncestral`, new) in a deck, enforced where cards are taken (`canAdd`). A run whose deck already holds more keeps them but is offered no more, and a Vault keeps such a deck (`checkEntry`, which the Vault and the ghosts use, checks copies only: `canAdd(..., false)`). **The Arena's doors enforce them** (`checkArenaEntry`: the queue, a ghost match, joining a room, and the Arena screen beforehand), with a message that says why; a ghost plays as it was saved. Events that trade a card use a new `swap` step that never raises the count of a rarity, so a deck with two Legendaries can still trade one at the reliquary; the random `exchange` no longer takes a Legendary or Ancestral card.
    - **Seven new events** (`eventsLegend.ts`): **the reliquary of legends** (needs a Legendary: give one of yours up for another of your own class or one of two Rift-crossed Legendaries, upgraded if yours was, or walk away keeping it), the bargain of echoes (copy a chosen card for two Static, or a random one for 6 HP), the fading saint (a random Boon for a full heal and +6 max HP, a potion for 30%, or 10%), the mirror of fates (half a chance of three upgrades or two cards changed; or break it for 100 shards), the Rift-crossed pilgrim (trade a card, or 1 of 3 Rift-crossed rares for 12% HP), the hungering blade (no walking away: HP for a chosen upgrade, or a Wound for shards), the smuggler's cache (1 of 3 Rift-crossed uncommons and shards, half a chance of 15 HP). 89 events in all.
    - **Ghost Wardens on the map:** a Warden that will be a ghost shows a floating ghost badge and is labelled "Ghost Warden" (with a line in the map legend and its screen-reader label). If the server has no ghost to give, the usual Warden is fought, as before.
239. **Fight recaps, replays, watching and run history (Robert, 2026-10-26: "lets add the fight replay and death recap. it would also be nice to review the battle log before leaving the match"; "spectating and sharing arena matches"; "a run history page") →**
    - **Recap:** a fight is played again from its setup and moves (`apps/web/src/fight/recap.ts`, pure) and added up: damage dealt, HP lost by source (a Trap, an enemy's move, a card), the blow that ended it, your three best turns, and the whole battle log. A lost fight's result says in one line what ended it; **Recap and battle log** opens the rest, and **Look at the board** sets the result aside to read the board and live log.
    - **Replay:** the fight screen has a watching mode (`watch`): moves arrive from a feed, nobody may act, with Pause and 1×/2×/4×. Every fight records its moves (both seats), so the result offers **Watch the replay** (not between games of a best of three, where the next game's clock runs).
    - **Run history** (`#/history`, this browser only): every run that falls, is banked or is abandoned (after its first stop) is kept with its deck, Boons, how far it got and its last fight (the one that ended a fallen run), to watch again or recap. 40 runs; the newest 12 keep their fight.
    - **Arena replays:** each finished game (2+ moves) is kept on the server 30 days (`replays` table, at most 20,000) and the `end` message carries its id. `#/replay/ID` plays it back; a replay from another `RULES_VERSION` is refused rather than shown wrong. The History page's Arena tab lists this device's games with Watch and Copy link; the result has **Share the replay**.
    - **Watching live:** `#/watch/ROOM` (the **Invite to watch** button in a match copies it). A watcher joins the room with `{type:'watch'}`, gets what the players get, seen from the first seat, with that seat's hand, piles and Traps hidden so nothing can be passed to the other player (a modified client could still read the state: the known limit in protocol.ts). Up to 50 per room; watchers never take a seat and are not asked who goes first.
240. **Arena ranks (Robert: "arena ranked tiers with bronze/silver/gold/platinum/diamond") →** an Elo rating per player per season (`packages/pvp/src/ranks.ts`, kept in a `ratings` table): start 1000 (Bronze II); Bronze <1100, Silver 1100, Gold 1250, Platinum 1400, Diamond 1550+; three divisions of 50 below Diamond. K 40 for a season's first 10 games, then 24; a winner gains at least 1. **Only counted matches against people move it** (the board's anti-farming limits apply; ghost games are not ranked: rating against a bot means nothing). A season is a month (the monthly board's), and the next starts every rating halfway back to 1000. Shown on name tags, the who-goes-first cards, a **Ranked** leaderboard tab (players with 3+ games) and the Arena page. Called "ranks", not "tiers", so they are never confused with Rift Tiers. Not done yet: matchmaking by rating (with few players, everyone is paired after 20 s anyway) and season-end rewards.
241. **Seasons, the hook (Robert: "eventually i want seasonal themes ... you could build some of the infrastructure") →** `packages/pvp/src/seasons.ts`: `seasonAt(now)` gives the season (a month of the Arena board, the same one ranks reset on), its key ("2026-11"), name and end, and `SEASON_THEMES` (empty) is where a theme (name, blurb, colour, realm) goes; a themed season takes its name from it.
242. **Constructed decks, switched off (Robert: "an arena mode with full custom built decks with some limits ... easy enough for me to turn on/off ... they cant fight with them unless its in the designated arena mode which isnt built yet") →** `packages/pvp/src/constructed.ts`: the pool is a Riftwalker's own and Neutral cards, their basics and the Ancestral cards, plain versions only (no upgrades, rolls, engravings or Rift-crossed cards); 20 to 30 cards, 3 copies, 1 Legendary, 2 Ancestral (`CONSTRUCTED_RULES`). `#/constructed` builds and keeps up to 12 decks in the browser; Play is disabled. **The switch is `ARENA_MODES.constructed` in that file** (false). Turning it on today only changes the wording: the Constructed queue (server side: a mode on the queue message, `checkConstructed` at the door, a separate board) is still to build.
243. **A smarter merchant and synergy hints (Robert: "i like your idea for the smarter merchant and card removal"; "the card synergy hints in rewards") →** the merchant already removed one card a visit (40) and sold upgrades, so: each removal bought in a run makes the next dearer by 15, up to 100 (`removePrice`, `RunState.removals`); **one card on every table is 30% off** (`sale`); and a merchant keeps one card of the lane your deck leans on most (3+ cards of it) on the table. **Hints** (`synergy.ts`): an offered card (rewards, the merchant, events, the altar) says "Fits your Bulwark (4)" when the deck holds 2+ of its lane, or "New lane: Swarm" when the deck holds none of it while building another; nothing for basics, glue, Legendary and Ancestral cards.
244. **Dispel by a share (Robert, 2026-10-26: "evaluate the Dispell mechanic that lessens debuffs, maybe it lessen debuffs by a percentage ... the doom mechanic since it can stack way higher than others ... variants that are like 25% 50% and 100%") →** the `cleanse` op gains `pct`: every debuff loses `amount` stacks or `pct`% of its stacks (rounded up), whichever is more. **Shrug It Off** (common): 1 or 25%; **Steady Nerves** (uncommon): 2 or 50%; **Purging Light** (rare, Consumed) stays the full dispel. Upgrades still add one stack. Why not more 100% dispels: Doom (Fate) is Mire's whole plan in the Arena, and a cheap full dispel would answer it outright; a share keeps it a race. Doom is only put on players by player decks (the Arena, ghost Wardens), so this is mainly a PvP change.
245. **Rift Omens and enemy phases (Robert: "omens to every tier ... make sure they stack for every future tier ... every tier ... starting at tier 2, but they need to scale up"; "a 2nd phase for elites and bosses is a great idea ... early on") →** `packages/campaign/src/omens.ts`: one Omen per Rift Tier from II to XX, all of a tier's Omens in force at once, and each one's numbers grow with the tier you are in. II Second Wind (elites and Wardens rise at 50% HP: Barrier 15% of max HP +1% a tier to 35%, 1 Might +1 every 6 tiers), III Dearer Roads (merchant +10% → 30%), IV Hardened Hides (enemies 1 Thorns, +1 every 6), V Thin Rest (rest heals 20% → 40% less), VI Rallied Foes (an ordinary fight's strongest enemy rises at 50% with 10–20% Barrier), VII Lean Purses (fight shards −15% → 30%), VIII Ironclad (elites/Wardens 1 Armored, +1 every 6), IX Dread (start Faltering 1, 2 from XV), X Narrowed Fates (one card fewer in rewards, never below 2), XI Rift Static (a Static card in your draw pile each fight, 2 from XVIII), XII Rites (elites/Wardens 1 Ritual), XIII Brittle Wards (start Brittle 2, more deeper), XIV Swarming (elites bring another of their weakest), XV Exposed Soul (start Exposed 1, more deeper), XVI Third Phase (elites/Wardens rise again at 25%), XVII Vigilant (enemies 1 Steadfast), XVIII Warden's Fury (Wardens +10% damage), XIX Hunted (enemies' first attack +4/+6), XX The Rift Awakens (every enemy rises at 50%). **Engine (generic, not per card):** a PvE setup's `mods` give enemies starting statuses and `rise` phases; a unit with a phase stops at its threshold (the blow that crosses it goes no further, so burst cannot skip a phase), then gains the Barrier and Might and a `phase` event; `HeroSetup.statuses` start the hero with statuses. The HP bar shows a notch where a phase waits. Not changed: the tier table (#229). **Open question for Robert:** Omens stack on top of that steep curve, so deep tiers may now be too hard; the next sim pass should say whether to flatten the table from Tier X.
246. **Warden's Hearts (Robert: "im also ok with your warden heart idea") →** five Hearts (`HEARTS` in boons.ts, `heart: true`, never offered or sold as Boons): Ember (+1 Mana, draw 1 fewer), Glutton's (draw 1 more, −15 Max HP), Blood (+1 Mana, lose 3 HP each turn), Thorned (start with 6 Thorns and 2 Might, Exposed 2 turns), Warded (16 Barrier and Shielded, Brittle each turn). Two are offered beside the Warden's Gift **instead of** its card (card, Heart or nothing: no double reward). One at a time (`RunState.heart`): another gives the first back, undoing its Max HP change. Works in fights like a Boon; not in the Arena; not on a saved deck.
247. **Point Blank (Robert: "add Point Blank to kp's old twelve gauge if theres only one target that increases damage by 25%") →** a `damage` op's `lonePct`: when the op finds only one enemy it deals that much more (rounded). The Old Twelve Gauge (hits every enemy) has 25. With the tooltip keyword "Point Blank". `RULES_VERSION` 3 (cards and the engine changed).
248. **Presentation (Robert: notifications "should have a dismiss button and sometimes they are stuck behind other elements"; "the 0 and 1 look too similar"; attacks: "leave the character where they are but ... some kind of slash on the enemy target ... or a hammer pound"; dragging: "it should change to an arrow when they move sufficiently close enough to enemy targets") →** the run's messages are toasts (`ui/Toast.tsx`): one stack at the top of the page, above every modal, each with ×; error boxes on the Arena and board pages get × too. Card costs are set in Cinzel 900 (round 0, serifed 1). Melee attackers stay at their place: a wind-up and a short step, and the blow lands on the target (two crossing cuts for a slash, a hammer swung down with a ground ring for a smash, claw marks, a thrust, a flash for anything plain); shots and spells were already ranged. A card with several targets that is dragged above the hand turns into the targeting arrow (the card waits lifted where it was picked up) and is dropped on a target as before.
    - **After review (2026-10-26):** enemies now spend Vigor on their next attacking move (the Omen of the Hunted did nothing before); a blow that lands exactly on a phase threshold rises there; ghost Wardens get the hero's Omen statuses and Warden's Fury, but no phases or guards (those are for enemy units, not a hero deck); the rest option and upgrade and removal prices show the Omens; giving a Heart back restores Max HP, not HP; the run sim plays the Omens. Left as they are, to judge in play: Swarming can double a one-unit elite; only the strongest enemy of an elite fight rises; Second Wind can stop a point above a Warden's own 50% pattern switch; a campaign fight saved mid-way before this version restarts.
249. **Fight layout and the chance wheel (Robert, 2026-10-26: the aura list "covers hp or nameplate"; potions "by the mana"; buttons "view map first, then forfeit, then abandon"; buffs/debuffs "similar to the size of boons"; Boons "on the players side ... below the view map button"; "look ok on laptop screens and above"; an event "said 25% chance ... but then it did the coin flip, that was very confusing") →** your side column at the top left, under the top bar, holds your Boons and then your Auras (clear of your plate). The potion belt sits beside the Mana orb, and the hand's fan keeps clear of it. Statuses on plates are small round icons like the Boons, with their stacks in a corner badge. The campaign's top bar reads View map, Forfeit, Abandon run. Boon chips carry their own styles, so they look the same in the sandbox. Checked at 1366×768. **An event chance is a coin only when it is even (50%)**; any other chance is a wheel whose gold share is exactly the chance, stopping with gold (it went your way) or dark under the pointer.
250. **Patch notes page (Robert, 2026-10-26: "a patch notes page ... dont mention changes that could be exploited ... for my play testers ... readable, and isnt just a super long page") →** `#/patch-notes` (`apps/web/src/app/PatchNotesScreen.tsx`, notes in `patchNotes.ts`): versions down the side, one patch at a time with its notes grouped as New, Changed, Balance, Fixed (filter chips), a "Heads up" line where testers should know something, Older/Newer buttons; on a narrow window the versions run across the top. The home page links to it, marked New until the newest notes are opened. Written for players and kept separate from CHANGELOG.md: nothing about server limits, anti-farming or anti-cheat checks, how boards decide what counts, hidden odds, or what a browser could see; a test fails if such words creep in, and another if the top entry is not the package version (so every deploy adds its notes). The home menu was tightened so it fits a 768px-tall laptop screen.
251. **One Arena, with ranks; reading width (Robert, 2026-10-26: "my desire is for Ranked to simply be Arena ... i want players to only have 1 option if they want to pvp, so might as well have a rank associated with it"; "it should only count for true pvp, not player vs ghost"; "text that spans the full width of the page again") →** nothing changes in the rules (#240 already ranked every counted match between people and never a ghost match); the presentation does: no separate "Ranked" tab, the Leaderboards' Arena tab opens on "Ranks this season" with "Wins this week" and "Wins this month" (the trophy races) beside it, the Arena page always shows your rank (a newcomer at Bronze II) and says that every match against a person counts and ghosts never do, and badges say "Arena matches". Reading width: a zero-specificity rule in global.css holds every paragraph in a `<main>` to 75ch, the board notes have their own width, and the character pickers use `auto-fill` so a realm with one Riftwalker does not stretch its card across the page; a sweep of every page at 1920×1080 found no text wider than 820px after this.
252. **Leaderboards layout and centred introductions (Robert, 2026-10-26: "the arena page has some mis-aligned text at the top"; the leaderboards are "just not the most intuitive") →** the Leaderboards page is three choices in order: a board (three cards: Arena, Campaign, Ghosts, each with a line saying what it ranks), its view (one segmented bar: Season ranks, Wins this week, Wins this month for the Arena; This week, This month for the others) with the time left on the right, and the table, with your own place above it; the explanations moved into a "How this board works" fold, and everything is one width (54rem). The misaligned text was a paragraph narrower than its centred header sitting at the left: every `.setup__lead` is now centred (`margin: 0 auto`), as are status-panel paragraphs, and the rank card is a compact centred card. The wide-screen sweep now also flags centred text that sits off the middle of its container.
253. **Ghost Wardens in range, settled up front; no Vault code box or server field (Robert, 2026-10-26: ghosts "never a tier higher than the player ... within 3 tiers if the ghost is below ... if there are no valid ghosts, then dont offer them as fights"; "why do we still have the vault code stuff"; "the server input on arena, i dont see why we need that") →** `pickGhostBoss` only takes decks from the player's tier down to `GHOST_BOSS_TIERS_BELOW` (3) below, never above, and returns none when nothing is in range; the campaign checks the same range on whatever arrives. The ghost is now settled when the stage begins (`ghostToSettle` / `settleGhost`, kept on the node as `ghostDeck`): the map shows "Ghost Warden" only once one is settled, and with none (or no connection) the node is the usual Warden. A save from before still asks as the fight begins, as it did. The Vault code box (a key typed on another device to load decks) is gone from the Arena page, since signing in does the same; the browser still keeps its own key in the background, so nothing anyone has saved is lost. The server address field is gone too; an address saved in a browser is now only honoured in a development build.
254. **Your username on the home page (Robert, 2026-10-26: "instead of the signed in text at the top of homepage, lets add the players username there if its set, and instead have some kind of short wording to have them set a user name ... we are going to use steam profile at some point") →** the home page's top right is a player chip (`apps/web/src/app/PlayerChip.tsx`): your username with its initial, or a dashed "Choose a username" prompt. Either opens a small panel with the username form (the same unique-name claim the boards use) and, below it, signing in or out with Google. For Steam: the chip is where the Steam persona name will show (the name will come from the Steam profile, see the Steam usernames memory and docs/12_STEAM_READINESS.md), and the change form is the part a Steam build drops.
255. **Build paths, traits for every path, no copied cards (Robert, 2026-10-26: "i dont like how our 'lanes' say hawk bear and elder ... talk in terms of 'build paths' instead of lanes"; "I want the build paths to be those visible tags when picking a character"; "review cards that are the near identical copies since i dont want to violate IP of other games, specifically slay the spire 2"; "some of the traits simply arent flexible enough to help the character in all 3 build paths ... like sylas, bleed is only one build path, so why is bleeding his trait?") →** "lane" is now **build path** everywhere: code (`Riftwalker.buildPaths`, `pathOf`, `pathName`, `pathSizes`, `paths.generated.ts`, `pnpm paths`) and player text; the build paths are the tags on the character picker. **A build path passes when** it has its own verb (something you do on your turn the other two don't), its own payoff cards, and a name that says how you play, not what you collect. **Ilvane** failed (Hawk/Bear/Elder were three flavours of one plan): now **Totems** (plant, Spirit, slots, awaken), **Whittling** (Carvings: 0-cost cards tagged `carving` that come back from the discard pile; new generic `fetch` tag filter) and **Rites** (Auras that pay when you play another Aura, and cards that count your Auras; new `powers` metric, which never counts a trait). **Sehra:** **Fury**, **Flow** (stance dancing: every stance change pays) and **Radiant**; Robert asked whether Fury is a real build path: it is, once Fury decks can survive the damage it lets in (Surge of Ire and Wrathful Guard give more Barrier, Inner Fire gives Barrier per stance). **Vesper:** Bluff (a mix of discard cards borrowed from Sylas) becomes **Patience**: Hold cards and leave Mana unspent, with payoffs for a full hand and for the Mana you kept. Everyone else's build paths already passed. **Traits must help all three build paths:** Sylas *Hidden Blades* (a Sleeve Knife every turn, used by Knives, by Bleed through Tainted Edges, and by Sleight as discard fodder; was 2 Bleed a turn), Vesper *Lying in Wait* (6 Barrier and 2 extra cards at the start, 4 Barrier a turn; was Exposed for 3 turns), Sehra *Inner Fire* (1 Fervor and 2 Barrier whenever she enters a stance; was 2 Fervor at the start), Dorian *Grave Chill* (3 Fate on every enemy at the start, and 3 on a random enemy whenever an ally dies or a card is consumed: Thralls die, Ashes consume; was 4 Fate at the start), Obsidia *Kindled* (also Sharpens the Obsidian Blade by 1 a turn, so the Blade build path gains from it). A trait can now carry triggers (`HeroTrait.triggers`): they fire like an Aura's but are never counted or listed as one. Valeria, Rivet, Kesh, Ilvane and KP keep theirs (each already helps every build path). **Original IP:** every card name that matched a Slay the Spire 1 or 2 card (about 50: the Watcher's in Sehra's pool, the Necrobinder's in Dorian's, Regent and Ironclad names elsewhere) is renamed, and cards that copied a famous card's effect and numbers (Echo Form, Creative AI, Machine Learning, Tempest, Glacier, Dualcast, Demon Form, Corruption twice, Offering, Impervious twice, Feel No Pain three times, Noxious Fumes twice, Catalyst, After Image twice, Acrobatics three times, Backflip, Blade Dance, Cloak and Dagger twice, Burst twice, Adrenaline, Bullet Time, Mental Fortress, Rushdown, Like Water, Ragnarok, Wheel Kick, Worship, Devotion, Scrawl, Time's Up, the Necrobinder's Soul and more) now work differently. Card ids never change (an id keeps a card's art and every saved deck), so a reworked card keeps its art; no card was added or removed. **Balance:** `pnpm audit:balance -- ladder --bypath` (new) shows every build path on its own row, and `pnpm traitscan` the value of each trait; results in BALANCE.md.
256. **Trades in events and at the Threshold (Robert, 2026-10-26: "some of our events/thresholds to include options targeted at uncommon attack cards or common skills ... replace 1 class specific rare card for another one or swap two common class attacks for 1 uncommon attack") →** five map events that trade particular cards of your own class, every card that leaves and every card that comes named on the option (`packages/campaign/src/eventsTrades.ts`): **The smelter** (two common Attacks melt into your choice of two uncommon Attacks), **The binder's table** (the same for Skills), **The fence** (a rare for your choice of two other rares, upgraded if yours was), **The specialist** (a common or uncommon from a build path the deck is not following becomes one of the same rarity for the path it is), **The master and the student** (an uncommon Attack or Skill and 60 shards for a rare of the same kind). Offers lean to the build path the deck follows (`mainPath`). A trade never takes a sanctified or engraved card (you paid for those). The Threshold gains four trades: **Trade Steel** (two Strikes for a choice of three uncommon Attacks), **Trade Guard** (two Defends for uncommon Skills), **Fresh Steel** and **Quiet Study** (one Strike or Defend for a choice of three upgraded common Attacks or Skills); the `pick` effect can now ask for one kind of card and `remove` for only Strikes or only Defends.
257. **Harder from Tier I, and a steeper climb (Robert, 2026-10-26: "id like the campaign tiers to scale a little bit harder a little bit faster ... honestly i feel like tier one baseline needs to be harder in general. more moster hp or something") →** the Rift Tier table (`packages/campaign/src/tierTable.ts`): Tier I enemies 150 / 168 / 186% HP and 124 / 130 / 136% damage (were 130 / 145 / 160 and 120 / 126 / 132), each tier to Tier X adds +55% HP and +20% damage (were +45 / +18), and Tiers XI-XX climb +18% HP and +6% damage a tier so the table stays under the 900% HP / 400% damage ceiling (Tier XX 864-876% HP, 379-383% damage; was 761-775 / 378-384). Mostly HP, not damage: more HP asks for a better deck, while more damage mostly punishes a bad draw. The table still only rises. Bot ladder, all ten Riftwalkers (`audit:balance -- ladder --decks 3`), before -> after: first Warden of a run 10% -> 6% (Elites 70% -> 46%, regular fights cost 25% -> 33% of HP), end of loop 1 Warden 35% -> 24%, Tier V Warden 54% -> 39% and Vorthax 38% -> 29%. People beat the bot by a wide margin, so these are floors.
258. **Thorns answer attacks only; one table for the Omens (Robert, 2026-10-26: "thorns should not hit the player if a trap did the damage. same with minions, the minion should receive the damage and not the player"; "i also want a place where i can easily change omen values") →** Thorns now hurt back only an attack: an Attack card, or a unit's own move (an enemy's, a companion's, which takes the Thorns itself, as it already did). A Trap, an Aura, a totem, a potion or a Skill that deals damage is no attack, so it no longer hurts its owner (it did: with the Tier IV Omen giving every enemy Thorns, a Trap or a totem hurt the hero on every hit). Tested in `mechanics.test.ts`. The Omens' numbers moved to `OMEN_TUNING` at the top of `packages/campaign/src/omens.ts`: for each Omen the tier it arrives at, its value there, how much it grows a tier and its most; the Omens, their text and the How to play page all read it. No value changed. Found on the way: two bots can deadlock in PvP (Valeria's Thorns stack past 500, the trapper bot will not hit them, the knight bot will not attack into a Trap); a person attacks, but PvP has no rule that ends a deadlock. Asked Robert whether to add one (sudden death from a late round); the PvP pairing test accepts that one stall meanwhile.
259. **PvP sudden death and a game clock (Robert, 2026-10-26: "after turn 50 then lets start taking away hp (dmg right through any shields) turn by turn. and a single battle should be called and finished at 20 minutes. pvp is 3 battles so that would mean each battle has 20 minutes") →** **Sudden death** (engine, PvP only: `SUDDEN_DEATH_AFTER = 50`, `SUDDEN_DEATH_STEP = 10` in `packages/combat/src/types.ts`): from Turn 51 (the turn number players see, both seats' turns together), the hero whose turn begins loses 10 HP more each Turn (10, 20, 30...), straight through Barrier, Shielded and Ghostly. Arena heroes have about 480 HP, so a stalled game ends within about ten more Turns. **The game clock** (`GAME_MS` = 20 minutes in `packages/pvp/src/protocol.ts`): each game of the best of three has its own 20 minutes from when it begins; then the server calls it for whoever has the larger share of their HP left (a share, so the second player's extra HP does not count), equal shares a draw (end reason `time`). The room's alarm wakes for it, the start message tells the browsers when (`gameEnds`), and the fight screen shows the game clock under the turn clock. `RULES_VERSION` 4: a game started under the old rules is ended rather than replayed. The PvP pairing test is strict again (the knight-trapper bot stall of #258 now ends in sudden death).
260. **Weapons on the stage, the played card by the discard pile, card backs on the piles (Robert, 2026-10-26: weapon animations "like kp with a hammer or shotgun ... their character model doesnt need to move, we just render the weapon with a brief animation"; "the card being played ui is right on top of the battle arena, lets move the card being played to the side by the discard pile. lets also add cards backs to the discard and consume piles") →** The weapon art is still pictures, one per weapon (`docs/weapon-prompts.md`, cut by `tools/pack-weapons.ps1` into `public/art/weapons` with an `index.json`), moved by the game, not frame-by-frame AI animation (an AI redraws an object a little differently in every frame, so it flickers). A hero's attack shows their weapon (`apps/web/src/fight/weaponArt.ts`): a melee weapon appears on the target and comes down across it (as the drawn effects did), with painted dust at the feet for a slam and sparks for a blade; KP's Old Twelve Gauge kicks in his hands with a painted muzzle flash; Sylas's knife flies spinning to the target; Ilvane's staff and Dorian's lantern lift with a painted spirit swirl. The weapon follows the card: KP fires the gun for gun and shell cards and anything that hits every enemy and swings the hammer for the rest; Valeria bashes with her shield for a card that hits with her Barrier. Without the files (or before they load) the drawn effects play as before. The card being played now shows in the right-hand column, over the battle log and just above End turn and the discard pile it drops into, never over the arena. Every pile (Draw, Discard, Consumed) wears a card back drawn in CSS (a gold frame, a lattice and a rift glow; Consumed burnt to ash).
261. **Every HP number in the tier table x1.5 (Robert, 2026-10-26: "please increase all of the numbers of hp in the tier table by 1.5x") →** done as asked: Tier I enemies 225 / 252 / 279% HP (were 150 / 168 / 186), Tier X 968-1022%, Tier XX 1296-1314%; damage unchanged. The table test's HP ceiling rose from 900% to 1350% to fit (the table still only rises). Bot ladder, all ten Riftwalkers, before (#257) -> after: first Warden of a run, regular fights 98% -> 91% won and cost 33% -> 50% of HP, Elites 46% -> 12%, Warden 6% -> 4% (25 turns); end of loop 1, Elites 70% -> 36%, Warden 24% -> 12%, Vorthax 11% -> 1%; Tier V, Elites 70% -> 46%, Warden 39% -> 26%, Vorthax 29% -> 20%. A big step: flagged to Robert as likely too much for Tier I (people beat the bot, but a regular fight costing half your HP early makes a run's first stage a slog).

262. **Tier HP and damage are flat climbs, set by six numbers (Robert, 2026-10-26: "lets bring down tier table hp to start at 150% and scaling up from there. im wondering if there is a flat% increase we can simply have for each tier up", then "lets do that same thing for the damage so i can easily chagne the numbers") →** the tier table is no longer typed row by row. At the top of `packages/campaign/src/tierTable.ts`: `HP_START` (150), `HP_PER_TIER` (+50 points a tier), `HP_PER_STAGE` (+15 a stage), and `DMG_START` (124), `DMG_PER_TIER` (+20), `DMG_PER_STAGE` (+6). HP: Tier I 150 / 165 / 180% (was 225 / 252 / 279), Tier X 600-630% (was 968-1022), Tier XX 1100-1130% (was 1296-1314). Damage keeps Tiers I-X exactly as they were and keeps climbing to 504-516% at Tier XX instead of flattening at 379-383% (the ceiling test rose from 400% to 550%; flagged). Per-tier must stay above twice per-stage so the ladder never steps back (the table test checks it). How to play reads the same function, so its table follows. (Also 2026-10-26: the ghost pilot now ends its turn in its head before judging a line, so allies, Traps and end-of-turn effects count, and a choice left open is answered first; a quick check against the old pilot on the starter decks won 111 of 200 seat-for-seat. Legendary Boons are crimson-ember instead of gold, so they never read as common.)
263. **Robert tuned the tier numbers (2026-10-26) →** HP_START 175, HP_PER_TIER 50, HP_PER_STAGE 10; DMG_START 120, DMG_PER_TIER 15, DMG_PER_STAGE 5. Tier I is 175 / 185 / 195% HP and 120 / 125 / 130% damage; Tier XX is 1125-1145% HP and 405-415% damage.
264. **Robert retuned the tier numbers again (2026-10-26) →** HP_START 150, HP_PER_TIER 50, HP_PER_STAGE 12; DMG_START 100, DMG_PER_TIER 20, DMG_PER_STAGE 5. Tier I is 150 / 162 / 174% HP and 100 / 105 / 110% damage; Tier XX is 1100-1124% HP and 480-490% damage. (Same day: allies show their end-of-turn action like an intent; the map names only Wardens; a campaign ghost never names its owner.)
265. **Multi-hit attacks keep going after their target dies (Robert, 2026-10-26: "if 2 of the 3 hit are able to kill my minion, then his last hit should still hit me. make sure thats working") →** it already worked (the hits left find a new target: a minion still standing, then the hero), but only by the default rule. Now an enemy re-picks by its own targeting rule (random or lowest HP), and a test checks the exact case: an 8x3 attack kills a 12 HP minion in two hits and the third lands on the hero for 8. (A recorded fight in which a random-targeting enemy killed a minion mid-attack could replay differently; no PvP effect, as Arena units have no enemy brains.)
266. **Robert lowered HP_START to 125 (2026-10-26) →** Tier I enemies 125 / 137 / 149% HP; Tier XX 1075-1099%. Everything else as in #264.
267. **No event forces a card (Robert, 2026-10-26: "players have a chance to walk away instead of having to choose to take a cards ... they shouldnt be forced to take a card especially from rift crossed options") →** the three cards an event lays out (crossedPick: the smuggler's cache and the other-worlds events) can all be left: `pickEventCard(run, null)` and a Take none button. What the option cost (HP, shards, a Boon) stays paid, as with skipping any card reward after paying for the fight. A test runs all 94 events and checks each has a way through that adds no card of your choosing (Wounds and Static are costs, not cards you take); all already did, the forced pick was only the three-card choice.
268. **Arena games are called at 15 minutes (Robert, 2026-10-26: "change the arena battle length to 15 minutes instead of 20 minute max") →** `GAME_MS` in packages/pvp/src/protocol.ts is 15 minutes (was 20, #259). Each of the three games of a match has its own 15; the call is unchanged (larger share of HP left). The server sends the end time with each game, so the client needs no change.
269. **Card reward odds, and card luck removed (Robert, 2026-10-26) →** per card offered: a fight 55% common / 35% uncommon / 9% rare / 1% Legendary (was 65 / 28 / 7 / 0), an Elite 35 / 42 / 20 / 3 (was 30 / 50 / 19 / 1). Robert: Slay the Spire (3% rare from a hallway fight) is the yardstick, but it has three rarities to our four, so Legendary takes 1% of a fight and rare is about 9-10%. The merchant rolls its stock at the Elite odds, so a Legendary now turns up on a shelf more often. Card luck (Lucky / Blessed) was no longer a choice, so it is gone from the code: one table, `CARD_ODDS`; the Warden's Gift has one Legendary chance (30%); an old save that still names a luck just drops it. How to play shows the one table.
270. **Giving up mid-fight counts as falling; "save" not "bank" (Robert, 2026-10-26: "a deck should be removed from the arena deck options if the player died in the campaign run. if the player abandoned the run while not in the middle of a fight, then the deck can remain valid for arena. i want there to be a trade of between going further in campaign trying to maximize a deck and just saving it for arena") →** a collapse already took the run's deck out of the Vault (#231). Abandoning did not, even in the middle of a fight, so a losing fight could be abandoned to keep the deck. Now abandoning a run whose phase is a fight (in the run, or from the home page) removes its Arena deck like a fall; abandoning anywhere else keeps the deck as last saved, and both confirmations say which. Every player-facing "bank" about decks now says "save" (Robert: card names like Banked Breath are fine). The code keeps its names (phase 'banked', bankedAt) so saves still load.
271. **Some upgrades make a card cheaper, not bigger (Robert, 2026-10-26) →** a big, one-off card gained little from the automatic +3 damage, so its upgrade now lowers what it costs: The Twelve-Gauge Opera+, Rift Collapse+, Bloodmoon Hunt+, The Great Pole+ and The Perfect Trap+ cost 2 Mana (were 3); Shatterstorm+ needs 4 Arcana (was 5), Cataclysm+ 3 (was 4), Killing Glass+ 2 (was 3); Cataclysm Totem+ awakens your totems 3 times (was 2) instead of +3 damage. The Perfect Trap at 2 Mana was flagged as possibly too easy to keep up; watch it.
272. **Cards that grow as you get hurt read the share of HP missing, not the amount (Robert, 2026-10-26: Bar Stool and the like "can go crazy in arena where we increase player health") →** Arena heroes have 6x HP (ARENA_HP_MULT), so "+1 per 5 missing HP" gave Bar Stool +80 near the end of a game, and "Barrier equal to 20% of your missing HP" let a hurt KP stall. New metric `missingPct` (0-100, of the unit's max HP), and all 17 cards moved to it at about the same value in the campaign (worked out from the owner's max HP): Bar Stool 10 +1 per 6% HP missing; Pane Up 6 (+1 per 11%), Window Pane 7 (8%), Pane in the Face 5 (6%), Double Glazing 12 (6%), Smash the Pane 8 (4%), Stained Glass 3 (11%) a turn, Panorama Window 14 (4%), Broken Window Theory 1 (+1 per 6%) to all, The Glass Palace 6 (6%), Last Orders 8 (4%); Hot-Blooded 8 (6%); Pain Ward 5 (5%), Maddened Lunge 5 (5%), Death or Glory 10 (2.5%); Last Stand 8 (2.5%), Sunforged Edge 8 (5%). The Barrier cards had a flat part and a scaled part; they are one effect now, so their upgrade adds 3 Barrier (was 3 to each part). Scaled amounts get a tiny rounding allowance (1/11 of 55 is 5).
273. **Random cards are shown before you choose (Robert, 2026-10-26: "any of these random card prompts to show the player the random cards its talking about ... what random card will be replaced and by what") →** every random card an event option means is picked when the event is laid out, and the option shows the cards: what leaves (or is upgraded), an arrow, and what you get. Data-driven events: `pinRemovals` now also pins trades (`exchange` becomes a `swap`), transforms, copies, gifts and Rift-crossed gifts (they become `card`), and records `EventOption.shows` {lose, gain} (only the sure ones, not those behind a chance). Hand-written events (one or two random upgrades, the mirror copy, scrap, purge, the transform wall) pick in `pinOptions` and `resolveEvent` uses exactly those cards (an older save without them picks on the spot, as before). Saves check `shows` holds real cards.
274. **A lost Warden costs 1 Resolve, and every Warden fight says what is at stake (Robert, 2026-10-26: "lets not lose 2 resolve on the boss fight for now. lets also warn on every boss fight about how much resolve the player has left") →** `resolveCost(kind)` in run.ts: 1 for a plain fight or a Warden, 2 for an Elite (was 2 for both). A Normal run (2 Resolve) now survives one lost Warden. Every Warden fight shows a line under the title for the whole fight: the Resolve left and what a loss would leave, in red when a loss would collapse the run. Also: Ammo Dump adds 6 Buckshot Shells (was 8), Ammo Dump+ 6 upgraded ones (6 damage to all; tokens can now have a hand-written upgrade); Fire in the Hole+ scales +2 per 3 consumed cards (base +1 per 2; Robert: the upgrade should reward scaling more than base damage, but not too fast). Card text can read a fraction ("+2 per 3") and plurals ("per 2 consumed cards").
275. **Card-shaped piles and one left column (Robert, 2026-10-26: "the draw/discard/consume piles to have the same aspect ratio as the actual cards ... the potions above mana doesnt seem exactly centered") →** the piles are 5.2rem x 7.28rem, the cards' own 5:7 (750 x 1050), so a pile is a stack of card backs. Potions, Mana and the draw pile share one centre line, 5.2rem wide from 1.4rem in (they were 3-5px apart); Mana, End turn, Arcana, the log and the card being played move up by what the piles grew (1.7rem).
277. **A saved run that cannot be loaded is shown, not hidden (a player could not start a third run with two listed) →** the 3 run slots count every saved run, and a save this build cannot read was left out of the home page list while still taking a slot, so the player saw 2 runs and New Run refused. The home page now lists such a save (`unreadableRuns` in saves.ts) with Copy save (for a bug report) and Remove (two clicks). Nothing is thrown away on its own. A test checks a run standing in every event survives a save, so the new event cards are not the cause.
278. **Up to 10 runs in progress (Robert, 2026-10-26: "increase the number of live active runs to 10 for now") →** `RUN_SLOTS` 10 (was 3). On the home page one or two runs are big Continue buttons as before; more become a compact list that scrolls, headed "Runs in progress · N of 10". The leaderboard still counts at most 10 new runs a day per player (NEW_RUNS_PER_DAY); that never stopped a run from starting.
279. **Replays fit for hunting bugs (Robert, 2026-10-26: "make sure the share replay functionality works well. i want it so i can check matches for bugs") →** (a) a game the clock called (reason time) could not be watched again: the replay check did not know that ending, and the page blamed the server. Fixed. (b) a replay is played again with today's cards, and only a rules version number guarded that, so an older game could play out differently with no warning, which would send a bug hunt after things that never happened. The server now keeps the game's fingerprint after every move in the replay, and the replay checks each one: from the first move that does not match it says so ("From move N this replay no longer matches the real game"). Replays saved before this have no fingerprints and play as before. (c) Replay controls: a move counter (Move 12 / 87) and a Step button while paused (one move, then hold). Also 2026-10-26: a held merchant card shows its next-time price; Traps are triggered, not sprung; coloured arrows replace the up / down triangles.
280. **A ghost Warden is never your own deck, however you were known when you saved it (Robert, 2026-10-26: "a player shouldnt get matched against their own ghost in the campaign") →** the server already skipped decks owned by the asker, but a player has two board ids (one from their signed-in account, one from their Vault code), and a deck saved under one looked like someone else's when they asked under the other (signing in later, or another device). Now the request carries every id the player can speak for and their username (unique, claimed), and pickGhostBoss skips a deck that matches any of them. Also: the weekly campaign board counts a run in the week it began (so a run carried across a Monday is not counted twice); Sacagaweeda's runs began before this week, so they show on the month board only.
281. **The campaign board shows every run that made progress in the period (Robert, 2026-10-26: "id like the weekly to show anyone who made progress this week") →** the board read runs by when they began (first_at), so a run carried across a Monday vanished from the week board. Now it reads runs by their last real progress (at, which only moves when a run gets further), and ranks them by how far they have got in all, as before. A long run shows on every week it moved in.
282. **The other side's column stacks, traits are on show, Apex Maw+ costs 2 (Robert, 2026-10-26) →** in the Arena the opponent's piles box and the column of their Boons and Auras were pinned to the same top-right corner, so the Auras covered the piles. The piles box is now the first thing in that column, then the opponent's trait, Omens, Boons and Auras (a long Aura list scrolls), so nothing overlaps; not draggable (a window players move needs saving per screen size and fights touch screens), stacked instead. Each side shows its Riftwalker's innate trait as a chip (hover for the text). Apex Maw+ costs 2 (was a flat +3); +4 per Might was tried and is past the guard rail for a consumed card (3 per point).
283. **Spin To Win: the first spin is free (Robert, 2026-10-26) →** the Brass Reel is called Spin To Win (the elite events and the Gambler's Shrine), and the first spin at a reel costs nothing (`spinCost`); each spin after it costs ROLL_COST (25).
284. **The Spin To Win reel always turns (a Mac player saw no spin, twice) →** with the system's Reduce motion on (common on a Mac: System Settings, Accessibility, Display) the reel jumped straight to the prize. Now it still turns, briefly and gently (1.4 s instead of 5.2 s). The slide also starts two frames after the jump to its start, since Safari can drop a transition started in the same frame as a style change; a fallback still shows the prize if the browser never says the slide ended.
285. **Kesh reworked; Lifesteal and companions that grow (Robert, 2026-10-26: "kesh is weak especially with basic/common/uncommon cards. if a card is going to cost 2 hp, it needs to have more benefit than just 2 damage more than a regular strike ... her trait should be her might applies to her allies, and she heals after matches"; "lets just introduce the mechanic called Lifesteal and use it as percentage of damage dealt"; "some of our characters with minions [should] be able to increase their minions life instead of only barriers") →** judged by numbers against the other Riftwalkers, not a sim: a 1-Mana common attack elsewhere is 7-8 damage with a small rider and costs no HP, so every HP one of hers pays now buys about 3 damage or Barrier, or something lasting. **Engine:** a damage effect can carry `lifesteal` (percent of the HP the attack took off, Barrier it hit does not count); a new `growHp` effect gives companions (never a hero: their HP belongs to the run) more max HP and heals as much; a trait can `shareMight` (every Might the hero gains or loses, their companions do too, and a new companion arrives with it) and `healAfterFight`. **Kesh:** Pack Instinct shares her Might, starts her with 1 and heals 6 after every won fight. Starter: 4 Strikes, 3 Defends, Savage Bite (9, Lifesteal 50%, no HP cost), Blood Frenzy (now her area attack: 4 to all, lose 1, 1 Might) and Guard Dog (moved to basic: a Hound and 5 Barrier). Commons that were near-copies were pulled apart: Rend 13/lose 2, Twin Fang 5x2/lose 1 (the Might multiplier), Ripping Claws 3x2 to all/lose 1, Blood Rush 5, Open a Vein (the Might one: lose 2, 2 Might), Feral Instinct (the draw one), Bloodied Howl lose 1 and draws, Thick Hide 6, Primal Feast heals 7, Marrow Suck Lifesteal 100%, Heel's Hound acts at once, Loose the Hound and Feeding Time grow companions. Uncommons: Ravage 9 to all/lose 2, Red Haze lose 3, Blood Bond to the pack; Devour, Fell the Prey and Gore and Gulp heal by Lifesteal 50%; Apex Maw Lifesteal 75%. Her "Might to all friends" cards say "Might" now (the trait shares it). **Companions that grow:** Rivet's Weld Armor (+6 max HP and 6 Barrier), Dorian's Grim Mending (+8 max HP, 4 Barrier) and Shared Marrow (companions +4 max HP, 3 Barrier to all).

286. **The merchant has one shape every visit (Robert, 2026-10-26: "the layout of the potions/boons/services seems to change ... the leave shop can be in the same position every time") →** Leave the shop sits in the header beside the purse; the cards are a little smaller (a full table fits one row); below them always three columns in a fixed order, Potions (with your own potions to sell as short lines under them), Boon and Services, each there even with nothing to sell ("No Boon today"). It used to be a flowing grid of up to four optional blocks with Leave at the bottom of the page.
287. **Review fixes for #285, and RULES_VERSION 5 →** RULES_VERSION was still 4 though cards changed from 0.71 on; it is 5 now, so a browser on an older build is told to reload and an Arena game saved under the old rules is ended rather than replayed differently (a rules change, the 0.84 move fingerprints aside, is when it goes up). Might sharing: Might for all friends on a side whose hero shares it goes to the hero only and is passed on (companions used to get it twice from another Riftwalker's card), and a loss passes on only what the hero really lost. A Lifesteal hit that wins the fight still heals. The checks refuse a negative max-HP growth and a Lifesteal outside 1-100%. A potion you are selling says what it does on hover.
288. **Obsidia: Arcana that pays on its own, and payoffs for spending it (Robert, 2026-10-26: Arcane Bolt "seems a little strong"; "she should also have some cards that cost only arcana"; keep the options of Slay the Spire 2's Regent in mind, without copies, repurposing cards that are useless or too similar) →** Arcane Bolt deals 12 (was 16). Cards paid in Arcana alone, so stored Arcana does something on a turn with no Mana: Spellbolt 2 Arcana for 12 (was 1 Mana + 1 for 20, stronger than her basic), Arcane Barrier 2 Arcana for 10 Barrier, Falling Shards 4 Arcana for 8x3 to all, Searing Ray 1 Arcana for 6 and 1 Exposed (was a plain 9, weaker than Emberstrike), Crown Strike 3 Arcana for 18 (was weaker than the common Blacksteel). Honed Strike makes Arcana (5 damage, 2 Arcana; was a weaker Forged in Fury). A new trigger, Whenever you spend Arcana (`arcana`, fired when a card's Arcana cost is paid): Sigil of Scorch deals 3 to all enemies on it (was a plain 3 each turn), Sigil of Glass gives 4 Barrier on it (was too close to Sigil of Stone). Compared with the Regent: generating Stars, Stars-only cards, Forge and the Blade, Mana now or later were already there; what was missing was payoffs for spending them, and that is what these add.
289. **A lost fight is retried with at least half your HP (Robert, 2026-10-26: "lets put the player at 50% hp after resolve") →** a fight lost with Resolve left used to be retried at exactly the HP you entered it with, so going into a Warden low meant retrying it just as low. Now the retry starts at that HP or half your max HP, whichever is more (`RETRY_HP_PCT` 50 in run.ts); nobody is ever set lower than they came in.
290. **The fight result leads with Continue (Robert, 2026-10-26) →** Continue stands on its own, large, on the left (and first for the keyboard); Recap and battle log, Look at the board and Watch the replay are stacked beside it as quieter buttons with room between them. Also: the Sharpen number on Obsidia's chip was dark brown on its dark badge once the blade was sharpened, so it could not be read; it is gold now.
291. **The campaign board shows each player once per Riftwalker, at the furthest (Robert, 2026-10-26) →** rows were grouped by the player's board id, and a player can report runs under two ids (their signed-in account and their Vault code), which would list the same Riftwalker twice under one name. They are grouped by username now (usernames are unique and claimed) and by the canonical Riftwalker id (an old id like mire counts as necromancer), keeping the furthest run.
292. **The campaign board says how each run stands (Robert, 2026-10-26: "display the status of the run, whether its still live or how it ended") →** a run on the board is Live, Fell or Abandoned. The server's runs table has an `ended` column (null while the run goes on); the game reports the end through the same run report with `ended`: fell when Resolve runs out or the run is given up mid-fight, abandoned when it is given up anywhere else (in the run or from the home page). Only a run the board already knows is marked, and only once. A run that ended before this build stays Live on the board (nothing told it otherwise).293. **One Legendary Boon per run (Robert, 2026-10-27: "only one legendary boon per run") →** `holdsLegendBoon` in packages/campaign/src/rewards.ts: once a run holds a Legendary Boon (Warden's Hearts are not Boons and do not count), `offerBoon` treats every offer as a shop's (a Legendary roll becomes a rare) and `boonOfRarity` leaves Legendaries out, so no Elite, event, Threshold option or Vorthax clear gives a second. A run that already holds two keeps them. How to play says so.
294. **Ideas triage (Robert, 2026-10-27) →** yes: enemies that set Traps, more Boons (drafts for Robert to review first), Warden signature cards "or some kind of variant", per-Riftwalker mastery (card backs that glow a new colour every 5 Tiers; other rewards undecided, and kept small so cosmetics stay worth buying). Later: a Daily Rift, a guided first fight; music is Robert's. No: a Codex (the How to play pages are the glossary), a draft Arena (the Arena is only for campaign decks). Proposals and the kept ideas are in `docs/13_PROPOSALS.md`.
295. **No Constructed decks; Arena only takes campaign decks (Robert, 2026-10-27: "i dont want it so make sure they cant take custom built decks in to arena") →** reverses #242: `#/constructed`, its screen, its saved decks and `packages/pvp/src/constructed.ts` (`ARENA_MODES`, `CONSTRUCTED_RULES`, `checkConstructed`) are deleted, and the Arena page no longer links to it. The only code that saves an Arena deck is the campaign's (run/deckSave.ts, run/BankedPanel.tsx); the sandbox never does. Limit: the server checks a deck's cards and limits (`checkArenaEntry`) but trusts that it came from a run, so a player who edits their browser storage could still send any legal deck. Closing that needs server-verified runs (12_STEAM_READINESS §6). Sandbox and DeckBuilder stay.
296. **Arena page: queue count and a Play a friend panel (Robert, 2026-10-27: "the room code input field doesnt fully show the placeholder text ... a count that shows how many players are queued for arena") →** `GET /pvp/queue/count` (worker.ts to the Matchmaker object) answers how many players hold a place in the queue; the Arena page asks every 15 s while idle and the tab is visible (`queueCount` in boardApi.ts) and shows "N players are looking for a match" with a green dot, or "Nobody is looking for a match right now"; nothing shows if the server cannot be reached. Make a room and the room code now sit in their own "Play a friend" panel (Make a room · or · code + Join, stacked on phones); the field is wider, its placeholder is "Room code" in normal letter spacing (the code typed in stays spaced), and Enter joins.
297. **Enemy Traps fully hidden; mastery by Tiers cleared (Robert, 2026-10-27) →** proposals updated in docs/13_PROPOSALS.md: an enemy Trap shows only that it is set (not its trigger or effect), "for now"; Riftwalker mastery counts the highest Tier cleared (its Vorthax beaten), shown on the character picker, a Mastery page and the Arena name plate.
298. **Kesh toned down early: minions by rarity, half her Might (Robert, 2026-10-27: "kesh feels too strong now early in the campaign, particually with the minion build ... the low level cards for minions to have lower hp, the higher rarity cards come with tankier minions"; "make kesh's trait pass down half of her might to her minions") →** three pack companions now, by the rarity of the card that calls them: **Pup** 6 HP, hits 3 (Guard Dog, basic; Heel, common), **Hound** 10 HP, hits 4 (Rally the Pack, uncommon; and the one she starts each fight with, unchanged), **Pack Alpha** 14 HP, hits 5 (Alpha Call, rare); Den Mother's Dire Wolf (18) and the Gnashlings (4) are unchanged. `HeroTrait.shareMight` is now a share (a number): companions hold that share of the hero's Might, rounded down, kept in step as it changes (a hero going 1 to 2 at half passes on 1), and a new companion arrives with it. Kesh's is 0.5 (was all of it). Pup and Pack Alpha use the Hound's figure for now.
299. **Auras can answer a reshuffle; Rift Resonance is a real Aura (Robert, 2026-10-27: Rift Resonance "say it applied 2 might, but its not clear if thats every round or just once. if its just once, then shouldnt that be a consume card instead of an aura? id even be ok with some auras only triggering when your deck gets re-shuffled") →** a new generic trigger `shuffle` ("Whenever your deck is reshuffled"): fires each time the discard pile becomes the draw pile (a draw or a scry that runs dry). Rift Resonance (Neutral rare, 2 Mana, was "gain 2 Might" once) is "Gain 1 Might. Whenever your deck is reshuffled: gain 1 Might." 18 other Auras still do their whole effect once when played (status Auras like Unchained, Radiant Oath, Rampart of Ages); which of those become Consume skills or reshuffle Auras is Robert's call.
300. **One-shot Auras say how long they last; statuses you gain read "Gain" (Robert, 2026-10-27: "move forward with your suggestion for those remaining auras. i will review them later") →** the 18 Auras that do their whole effect once when played stay Auras (Ilvane's Rites pays for every Aura played), but their text now says the status lasts: "Gain 3 Might for the rest of the fight." (one lasting status), or "These last the rest of the fight." (several). Lasting statuses: Might, Thorns, Bracing, Fortified, Cadence (ritual), Resonance, on yourself or all friends; Edge and maximum Mana already said so; Shielded is used up, so Hallowed Ground says nothing more. Separately, every card that puts a status on yourself now reads "Gain"/"Lose" instead of "Apply"/"Remove" (enemy moves keep their wording). Candidates for reshuffle Auras (#299) when Robert reviews: Radiant Oath, Unchained, Rampart of Ages.
301. **The battle log says how hard a move hits and how HP moved (Robert, 2026-10-27: "a squire's slash should show the number of damage in parenthesis like Slash (9). and it should show a target's hp and how it decreased/increased") →** engine events carry what the log needs (optional fields, so older replays still read): `act` has `damage`/`hits` (the move's first attack per hit, Might, Faltering, stances and the target's Exposed counted, the same figure its intent shows; left out for a move whose damage is a formula), and `damage` and `heal` have `hp` (the target's HP after). The log names an enemy's move by its real name ("Gilded Squire uses Slash (9)", "(4×3)" for several hits) and ends a hit or heal that changed HP with "(HP 40 → 34)". The Namer remembers each unit's definition for the move names.
302. **More maximum Mana starts next turn; Deep Pockets+ Holds and opens (Robert, 2026-10-27: "vespers Deep Pockets shouldnt reward mana on the current turn. the upgraded version shouldnt become cheaper, it should just add Hold and Opening Hand. its a huge thing to get 1 mana every turn so thats how i want to balance that type of card") →** the `maxMana` op no longer pays the extra Mana out on the turn it is played: it raises the maximum, and the Mana arrives from the next turn ("From your next turn, gain 1 more Mana every turn for the rest of the fight."). This is the op, so it holds for every such card (Deep Pockets, Attunement, Blood Pact, Last Call, Root Tap, The Long Memory), as "that type of card". Deep Pockets+ keeps its 3 cost and gains Hold and Opening hand (a custom upgrade) instead of costing 2.
303. **The upgrade shows on hover everywhere; the sandbox book runs by character (Robert, 2026-10-27: "theres no way to see card upgrades in card library or in my grimoire in the campaign. lets make sure we can see the upgrade on hover"; "the grimoire in sandbox should not be partiitioned by build path, and teh upgrade checkbox shouldnt need to refresh the grimoire") →** the big hover preview (CardHover) shows the "+" version beside the card by default wherever it is used (card library, campaign Grimoire, deck reports, sandbox book, pickers); only a card in your hand during a fight leaves it out. The sandbox book's pages run by character, then rarity, cost and name (`sortCards(..., 'character')`, `paginate(..., byPath false)`), with no build-path sections or headings; the card library and campaign Grimoire were already unsectioned. Ticking Upgraded (+) swaps the cards in place on the same pages, with no page turn.
304. **"Trap", never "Snare", and Trap is a highlighted keyword (Robert, 2026-10-27: "Gleeful Trapsmith that still refers to traps as Snares ... it should say trap. and since Trap(s) is a key mechanic, it should be highlighted with a tooltip on hover like the other mechanics") →** the rules text named card kinds from their code ids in two places ("Whenever you play a Snare", "Snare cards"); both now use the player-facing names (Trap, Aura). "Trap"/"Traps" is bold on cards like the other keywords, and its note (already in the keyword table) shows on hover for any card that names it. A test checks no card's text says "Snare". The card list writes trap and aura for those kinds. Code identifiers (`snare`, `SnareRow`) are unchanged.
305. **The campaign board's run state is a last column called Status (Robert, 2026-10-27) →** columns: Player, Character, Where they are, Stages cleared, Status (Live / Fell / Abandoned; #292). Also from the review of #301-303: an enemy's Vigor now counts in its intent's damage and in the battle log's "Slash (9)", the log's hit count adds up every attack in the move (as the intent does), the sandbox book keeps cards with no "+" version (tokens) when Upgraded is ticked, and the hover clears on that toggle.
306. **A Stunned hero draws one card fewer (Robert, 2026-10-27: Stunned in the Arena "isnt stunning players to miss a turn, and i dont think it should, but we need a better solution"; chose option A of five; "campaign should still stun enemies like normal except if its a ghost") →** a hero who is Stunned (an Arena opponent, a ghost Warden) draws one card fewer at the start of their next turn, and the Stun is spent; they still take the turn. Companions and campaign enemies skip their next action as before. Stunned still caps at 1, so it never stacks. Options not taken: a dead "Dazed" card in hand, the first card next turn costs 1 more, the hero's companions and Traps stunned instead, or Faltering in place of a Stun on a hero. The Stunned tooltip says both.
307. **Arena: queue first, then choose a deck before every game (Robert, 2026-10-27: "arena matches should allow users to queue up before selecting a deck ... they can choose which deck they want to battle with before each fight"; matched by Arena rank; picks blind; decks of any Riftwalker) →** the queue, ghost and join messages carry `decks` (every Arena deck the player holds, 1 to `MAX_MATCH_DECKS` = 10, checked by `checkArenaDecks`: one name and board id for all) instead of one `entry`. The matchmaker asks the board for the player's rating and `pairUp` pairs the closest ratings within `QUEUE_RATING_GAP` (100, about two divisions) until `QUEUE_WIDEN_MS`. A ghost is found for the player's deepest deck. In the room, the choose step before every game (the first included) now also takes a `pick` (an index into your decks; changeable until the game starts): the `choose` message carries your decks, your pick, the deck you would play, the opponent as a `FoeCard` (name, trophies, rank only) and whether they have picked. A game starts once the order is settled and both decks are chosen (`picksDone`: a player with one deck, or the computer, needs no pick), or when `PICK_MS` (30 s; 40 s between games) runs out, when an unpicked player plays the deck they played last, or their first (`deckToPlay`). The Arena lobby lists your decks without choosing; the choose panel shows them as buttons, the opponent as a hidden "?" with "Choosing a deck…" / "Deck chosen". `pnpm pvpsmoke` plays a match where both bring several decks and checks that the picks are played and nothing of the other's decks arrives before the game.
308. **Kesh's basics toned down (Robert, 2026-10-27: "nerf keshes hound to 8 hp"; "remove the guard dog basic card for kesh, its leading to too much minion block early on for free. lets move Rend down to basic and lessen its damage to 9, lose hp to 2, and add 1 might. and savage bite should be 8 damage with an upgrade of 2 dmg") →** the Hound has 8 HP (was 10; she still starts each fight with one, and Rally the Pack brings one). Her starting deck has Rend instead of Guard Dog. Rend is a basic: Deal 9, lose 2 HP, gain 1 Might; Rend+ (a custom upgrade, so a basic does not get the full rule's +3 damage, -1 HP and +1 Might) deals 12 with the rest the same. Savage Bite deals 8 (Lifesteal 50%), Savage Bite+ 10. Guard Dog is kept in the card table only so decks that already hold it still load; a basic is never offered.
309. **Runs follow the player between devices (Robert, 2026-10-27: "we need people who log into other devices to have their runs/data carry over to the other device. my friend logged on his phone and he didnt see his runs") →** only saved Arena decks were kept on the server (the Vault, under the account when signed in, else a Vault code); runs in progress lived only in the browser that played them. Now the same Vault keeps a copy of each run (`runs` table in `apps/server/src/vault.ts`; `POST /pvp/vault/runs` and `/runsave`). The browser brings its runs and the server's together (`apps/web/src/run/runCloud.ts`, merge rules in `packages/pvp/src/runsync.ts`): for each run the newest copy wins; a run that ended or was abandoned leaves a "gone" copy so other devices drop it too (kept 90 days). The home page syncs when it opens and when the window regains focus; a run sends itself up a few seconds after each save. Not yet carried over: the run history (ended runs and their replays), the Arena match history, and settings.
310. **Stun is Exposed, Faltering and Brittle on its own countdown, for everyone (Robert, 2026-10-27: "nerf the Stun mechanic to apply exposed/faltering/brittle instead ... on a different decrementing number so we can control it. the effects dont stack with the others"; for everyone; "1 turn unless the card says applies X stun, then it lasts for X turns") →** replaces #306 and the old skip. While a unit is Stunned it takes 50% more attack damage, deals 25% less and gains 25% less Barrier, exactly as Exposed, Faltering and Brittle do, but only once: a unit that is also Exposed (or Faltering, or Brittle) is not hit twice by the same effect (`modifiedDamage` and the Barrier op read "Exposed or Stunned", and so on). Nobody skips a turn or draws fewer cards: companions and enemies act, heroes draw a full hand. Stunned's stacks are its turns left; a new Stun keeps whichever is longer (`max`, never added), so Stuns cannot be chained into a lock. It counts down at the end of the stunned unit's own turn; one that lands during that unit's own turn (a Trap that answers its attack) is marked `stunFresh` and waits for the next turn's end, so it always covers one full turn of theirs. The bot weighs it as the three at once.
311. **Uncommons make allies act only when that is their point (Robert, 2026-10-27: Rally the Pack "should not include Allies act now, no uncommon card should have that unless thats the main mechanic it brings ... maybe instead it gives each ally 3 barrier") →** a new generic target `allAllies` (each of the actor's allies, never the hero; "Each of your allies gains 3 Barrier."). Rally the Pack: summon a Hound, each ally gains 3 Barrier. The same swap for the two other uncommons where allies acting was a side effect: Hamstring Slam (Kesh: Deal 11, Faltering 2) and Second Death (Mire: revive). Savage Command keeps it (acting is the card), and Rivet's uncommons keep it: making the machines act is his own mechanic (Assemble).
312. **Kesh pays 1 more HP everywhere; Heel, Howl and Guard Dog are gone (Robert, 2026-10-27: "all of keshs cards that read lose 1 hp should be lose 2 hp, and the others should increase by 1 as well. since she now has life steal she can handle it better. Howl and Heel cards for Kesh should be removed from the game, same with guard dog") →** every "lose N HP" in her pool is N+1 (34 of them, Auras and upgrades included: Rend now loses 3, Blood Pact 7, Twin Fang 2). Heel, Howl and Guard Dog are deleted. So that saves and decks made before still load, `RETIRED_CARDS` (packages/content/src/v2/index.ts) maps each to a replacement, keeping its roll, engraving and upgrade (`canonicalCard`): Guard Dog → Rend (her basic), Heel → Call Gnashlings (her common summon), Howl → Pack Tactics (a common that lifts the pack). A saved run is mapped as it loads (`parseRun`), and a saved Arena deck or ghost as it is checked (`checkEntry`). The Pup companion (only Guard Dog and Heel called it) is left in the unit table, unused.
313. **Review fixes for #310-312 →** a saved deck whose retired card's replacement would break a copy limit loses that one card instead of the whole deck being refused (and so silently dropped from the Vault); ghost Wardens and the run history read old card ids as their replacements (`checkGhostBoss`, history `read`), so old Kesh ghosts still turn up and old runs can still be watched; a re-Stun that does not lengthen the countdown no longer holds back that turn's tick. RULES_VERSION 9, version 0.92.0.
314. **Kesh's pack smaller again; Stun cards apply only Stun (Robert, 2026-10-27: "hound health ... to 5, savage bite damage to 6, upgrade to 8. hound dmg down to 3. feeding time hp increase to 3"; "for cards that apply stun plus another effect, just apply stun for now. if its a higher rarity card lets apply 2 stun") →** the Hound has 5 HP and hits for 3 (Hound+ 9 and 5); Savage Bite deals 6 (8 upgraded); Feeding Time gives each companion 3 max HP (5 upgraded). Stun cards: the debuffs Stun already covers are dropped (Override Virus loses its Exposed 2, Stun Tonic its Exposed 1); the rare and Legendary ones Stun for 2 turns (Override Virus, Silencing Trap, The Perfect Trap, The Long Ambush); EMP (uncommon), Whisper Net (common) and Stun Tonic stay at 1. Damage, Shielded and Cancel on those Traps are kept (read as "another debuff", not every effect). Card text reads "Stun the attacker for 2 turns." A Stun put on by a Trap that springs as its target's turn begins is not "fresh", so The Long Ambush lasts exactly 2 enemy turns.
315. **Kesh holds 2 companions at once (Robert, 2026-10-27: "make the hunter max minions to 2") →** `HeroDef.maxAllies` (generic: MAX_ALLIES, 3, when missing; `allyCap` in engine.ts) and Kesh's is 2. A summon past it asks for one companion to be released, as at 3. Litter (3 Gnashlings) brings as many as there is room for.
